import numpy as np, scipy.fft as fft? Might be available but if not, use numpy.fft. To be safe use numpy.fft. rfft.
def fftconvolve_square(a): n=len; L=1; while L<2*n-1: L<<=1; A=np.fft.rfft(a,L); c=np.fft.irfft(A*A, L)[:2*n-1]; return c.real. For convolution with w maybe general.
For gradient, need convolution of a_rev and w: length n + (2n-1) -1 =3n-1. Use rfft length L3 power2 >=3n-1. A=np.fft.rfft(a_rev, L3); W=np.fft.rfft(w, L3); g_full=np.fft.irfft(A*W, L3); grad=2*g_full[n-1:2*n-1].
But w length 2n-1. Need rfft w padded L3.
Compute logsumexp with stability: c max maybe ~1/n. beta large. c_shift=c - cmax; logC = cmax + log(sum(exp(beta c_shift))). Avoid overflow; beta c_shift ≤0. Objective = logC/beta.
Weights w=exp(beta c_shift)/sum_exp. We can normalize w as probability density? It sums 1. In derivative of logsumexp, w sums1. Good.
Gradient of f = w^T? ∇f = (1/β? Wait f = (1/β) log Z. ∂f/∂a_i = (1/β) (1/Z)∑ exp(βc_k) β ∂c_k/∂a_i = ∑ w_k ∂c_k = grad above (no /β). Good.

Projected gradient on simplex:
Given a, grad. We can do line search: for γ in [1e-3,...], v = a - γ*grad. Need project onto simplex P(v). But scaling? We maintain sum=1. Projecting changes objective maybe. Use backtracking Armijo: base f. For candidate projected, if f(candidate)<f(old) accept. Since objective scale invariant and f not convex, this is gradient descent. However projected gradient onto simplex with gradient in ambient space: The projection introduces components tangent to affine sum; only tangent gradient matters. But okay.

Projection onto simplex (nonnegative sum=1) for vector v length n:
If n small. Algorithm: sort v descending, find rho max t with (v_i - tau)>0 sum=1. Top-level function simplex_project(v): sorted_v = np.sort(v)[::-1]; cumsum; n candidates; find rho = largest i such that 1 + sorted_v[i]*i > cumsum_sorted[i] (0-index). tau = (cumsum[i]-1)/i+1; return np.clip(v - tau,0,None). Need ensure sum 1. For speed, sorting O(n log n) per line search maybe heavy. n=4500, line search 5 per iter, iters maybe thousands => maybe okay? 4500 log ~50k ops; 5k iterations =>250M sort comparisons in Python? Uses C sort fast but many. Could optimize: Since gradient may not require strict sum; we can renormalize after positive clipping: a_pos = np.maximum(v,0); if sum=0 use small; a_pos /= sum. This is not exact Euclidean projection but simpler and maintain nonneg sum; may be better (exponential scaling) but has different step. It can cause large scaling if some negative clipped. Could use this as "positive renormalization projection". It corresponds to gradient descent on log-space? Might be okay. But exact projection to simplex can kill zeros. Positive clipping+renormalize is easy O(n). Let's use for speed, maybe also optional exact every few.

Optimizer details:
State: n, loga shape n. We'll store a.
Function softmax_loga(loga): m=np.max(loga); exp=np.exp(loga-m); s=exp.sum(); return exp/s, exp? We need loga maybe unbounded. After update, can rescale loga to keep finite: subtract mean or max.
Objective f_smooth(a, beta): c=conv_square(a); maxc=c.max(); if maxc<=0 return -inf; shifted=beta*(c-maxc); exp=np.exp(shifted); Z=shifted.sum? use log; obj=maxc+np.log(exp.sum())/beta. weights=exp/exp.sum(). Return c, obj, weights.
Compute grad: w length Lc=2n-1. Need w_ext? For gradient convolution a_rev (length n) * w (length Lc). Use L=next_pow2(n + Lc -1) = next_pow2(3n-2). A_rev = rfft(a[::-1], L); W = rfft(w, L); g=np.fft.irfft(A_rev*W, L); grad=2*g[n-1:2*n-1] (length n). Ensure finite.
Update: loga = loga - step * grad. But grad can have constant component; renormalize removes. We need step scaling. Adaptive: compute current obj.    Stage 2: beta=1.5*n for next 20%.
   Stage 3: beta=5*n for next 20%.
   Stage 4: beta=20*n for rest.

      c, obj, w = smoothmax

   This is one eval per iter (plus update). Good.

```
def optimize_from_start(a0, deadline, rng, beta_factor=10.0, max_time=None, verbose=False):
    a = normalize(a0.copy())
    n=a.size
    loga = np.log(np.maximum(a,1e-300)); loga -= np.max(loga)
    step = 0.1*n
    min_step = 1e-6*n? if n small maybe 1e-6? use max(1e-8,1e-6*n)
    beta = n*beta_factor
    best_obj = smooth obj
    best_a = a.copy(); best_score = fast_score(a)
    fail=0; last_check=0
    it=0
    while time<deadline:
        # adjust beta based on it or time progress? If max_time known.
        c, obj, w = smooth_max(a,beta)
        if not finite: break
        grad = gradient_from_weights(a,w,n)
        # gradient tangent? subtract weighted mean? not necessary since softmax invariant. But to avoid constant, subtract mean(grad)? The log update invariant to adding constant to grad. We can subtract np.mean(grad) to reduce dynamic range.
        grad = grad - np.mean(grad)
        if not finite: break
        # If grad all zero, add noise.
        trial_log = loga - step*grad
        # prevent large dynamic range: trial_log -= np.max(trial_log)
        trial_log -= np.max(trial_log)
        a_trial = np.exp(trial_log); a_trial /= a_trial.sum()
        _, obj_trial, _ = smooth_max(a_trial,beta)
        if obj_trial < obj - 1e-12:
            a=a_trial; loga=trial_log; best_obj=obj; obj=obj_trial
            step = min(step*1.02, 0.5*n)
            fail=0
        else:
            # If minor increase, maybe accept with stochastic? no.
            step *=0.5
            fail +=1
            if step < max(1e-6, 1e-6*n? maybe 1e-8):
                # stuck: add noise or beta change
                loga += 0.1 * sqrt(n)? /? rng.normal; normalize
                # reset step
                step=0.1*n
                fail=0
                # maybe increase beta slowly
        it+=1
        if it % 20 ==0:
           sc=fast_score(a); if sc<best_score: best_score=sc; best_a=a.copy()
        # beta annealing: maybe beta = n*(beta_factor + it/1000)
    # zero small, exact local?
    return best_a,best_score
```
`smooth_max` returns c too. To avoid computing c twice per iter (obj and grad), use c,w. For obj_trial after update, need compute again. That's 2 FFT per iter. For n=4500 maybe okay but line search double. We can reduce: For update, compute trial by using same gradient approximate? Or accept without trial if step small? But need robust. For small m, okay. For large refinement, maybe we can use only smooth internal objective and not exact? Final objective not smooth. We can run large with fewer iters. Another approach: Use Adam-like with fixed step and no trial; compute new objective after step (1 FFT per iter). If objective increases, reduce step but the sequence already changed? We can rollback to previous loga. Need compute previous obj then trial obj: 2 FFT. But we already compute old c,obj from old a each iteration; after update we need trial. So 2. Could reuse c for old obj. Accept first improvement no line search; if fail, step reduce and retry with same old a (compute new trial) => additional. To limit, we can if fail, rollback without multiple trials: step*=0.5 but keep a old and retry next loop. That uses one trial. In loop: compute old c, grad; trial; if fail, don't update, reduce step, continue (so we recompute old c again next iter, wasting). Better line search within while.

Simplify for code: For small m use two evals; for large maybe use one (no rollback?) We can make `optimize_from_start` with parameter `line_search=True` for small; for large, use trust: step small enough that objective likely not horrible. But to get to target need step large. Hmm.

```
mvars
x = rng.uniform(-init_amp, init_amp) or sine modes.
a = make_a(x,n)
best = ...
for iter:
   c,obj,w=smooth_max(a,beta)
   ga=gradient_from_weights(a,w,n)
   gx=(ga[:m]-ga[m:])/n  # for even
   # Adam update:
   mt=beta1*mt+(1-beta1)*gx; vt=beta2*vt+(1-beta2)*gx^2
   x_new=x - lr*mt_hat/vt_hat
   x=np.clip(x_new,-1,1)
   renormalize? sum fixed.
   compute obj or score.
```
Line search? Adam with lr maybe n? gx scale? ga~1/n, /n =>1/n^2? Let's estimate. f~1/n, grad_a~? For c~1/n, derivative wrt a ~1/n? ga ~1/n. gx~1/n^2. Very small. `next_power_of_two(n)`:
```
def next_power_of_two(n):
    p=1
    while p < n: p <<= 1
    return p
```
Could use `1 << (int(n)-1).bit_length()` if n>1.

`conv_square_fast(a)`:
```
def conv_square_fast(a):
    a=np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n=a.size
    if n == 0: return np.array([])
    if n <= 256:
       return np.convolve(a,a)
    L=1 << max(1, (2*n-2).bit_length()) # since length 2n-1; bit_length of need-1?
    A=np.fft.rfft(a, L)
    c=np.fft.irfft(A*A, L)
    return c[:2*n-1]
```
For L need >=2n-1. Use `need=2*n-1; L=1; while L<need: L<<=1`.
`np.convolve` for n=256 direct length511 C fast. For n up to 512 maybe direct okay. Use threshold 512? Direct 512^2=262k, okay. For 4500, FFT. For local search n=4500, FFT.
We must ensure `np.fft` imported. Good.
Maybe using `scipy.fft.next_fast_len` improves but no need.

`score_fast(a)`:
```
def score_fast(a):
   a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64)
   s=float(np.sum(a))
   n=a.size
   if s < 0.01 or not all finite: return inf
   if n == 0: return inf
   c=conv_square_fast(a)
   # c may have tiny imag? irfft real. Use max
   mc=float(np.max(c)) if c.size else 0
   if not np.isfinite(mc) or mc < 0: mc=0
   if s <=0: return inf
   return float(2.0 * n * mc / (s*s))
```
Since scale invariant, we can use sum. If our a sum1 fine.
Potential issue: If a has negative tiny due to clip? We'll clip nonnegative before. Use `a=np.maximum(a,0)`.

`smooth_max(a, beta)`:
```
def smooth_max(a,beta):
    c=conv_square_fast(a)
    mc=float(np.max(c)) if c.size else 0.0
    if mc <= 0 or not finite: return c,0, np.ones_like(c)/len(c)
    # For large beta, shift
    beta=float(beta)
    x=beta*(c-mc)
    # x<=0 maybe; if some positive due rounding? clip x=np.minimum(x,0) maybe.
    # exp safe
    ex=np.exp(x)
    s=ex.sum()
    if s <=0 or not finite: # fallback top
       w=np.zeros_like(c); idx=np.argmax(c); w[idx]=1; return c, mc, w
    w=ex/s
    obj=mc + float(np.log(s))/beta
    return c,obj,w
```
If beta huge, x may underflow for all but max, fine.

`gradient_from_weights(a,w,n)` as above:
```
def gradient_from_weights(a,w):
    n=a.size
    Lc=w.size
    rev=a[::-1]
    need=n+Lc-1
    L=1; while L<need: L<<=1
    Ar=np.fft.rfft(rev, L)
    W=np.fft.rfft(w, L)
    g=np.fft.irfft(Ar*W, L)
    grad=2.0*g[n-1:2*n-1]
    grad=np.where(np.isfinite(grad), grad, 0.0)
    return grad
```
Let's verify slice length: n + Lc -1 where Lc=2n-1 => 3n-2? Wait need = n + Lc -1 = 3n-2? For n=3, need=3+5-1=7. Irfft length L. We want h_{i+n-1} for i=0..n-1, indices n-1 to 2n-2. length n. Good.
If n small use direct maybe? FFT okay.

`make_antipal_sequence(x,n)`:
```
def make_antipal_sequence(x,n):
    x=np.asarray(x,dtype=np.float64)
    a=np.empty(n,dtype=np.float64)
    if n%2==0:
       m=n//2
       # x length m
       a[:m]=(1.0 + x)/n
       # indices m..n-1 correspond j=n-1-i for i=m-1..0?
       a[m:]=(1.0 - x[::-1])/n
    else:
       m=(n-1)//2
       a[:m]=(1.0 + x)/n
       a[m]=1.0/n
       a[m+1:]=(1.0 - x[::-1])/n
    return np.maximum(a,0.0)
```
For even n, sum: sum first m (sum1+sumx)/n, second (m - sumx)/n => (2m)/n=1. Good. Values nonnegative if |x|≤1 after clip; use np.clip(x,-1,1) before.
For odd, middle 1/n, pair sums 2/n.

`project_to_antipal(a,n)`:
Given a normalized (sum1). For pairs i,j (i<j), want x_i = n*clip((a_i - a_j)/2, -1/n,1/n). Then sequence anti-pal. But maybe also adjust pair sums? We'll ignore pair sum deviations. Code:
```
def project_to_antipal_x(a,n):
   a=np.asarray(a); n=a.size; m=(n-1)//2? if even m=n//2; if odd m=(n-1)//2
   x=np.empty(m)
   for i in range(m):
      j=n-1-i
      d=0.5*(a[i]-a[j])
      x[i]=np.clip(n*d, -1.0, 1.0)
   return x
```
Loop O(n) fine. Could vectorize:
If even: first=a[:m], second=a[m:][::-1]; x=n*(first-second)/2 clipped.
If odd: first=a[:m], second=a[m+1:][::-1]; x...

`upsample_sequence(a, target_n)`:
Given p length m positive. Need build q length target_n. Use counts for each p_j. `base = target_n // m; rem = target_n % m`. counts = base+1 for first rem else base; ensure counts>0; if base=0 and m>target? If target_n < m, downsample by merging? We can handle target_n < m: maybe average groups? But we usually ups. If target_n==m return a. If target_n < m (not likely), we can downsample by averaging p into target_n bins (like block down), or just return p. For robustness:
```
if target_n == m: return a
if target_n > m:
  counts=...
  q=np.empty(target_n); pos=0; for j in m: cnt=counts[j]; val=a[j]/cnt; q[pos:pos+cnt]=val; pos+=cnt
  # sum approx 1; normalize
  s=q.sum; if s>0 q/=s
  return q
else:
  # use linear interpolation? simpler: choose indices floor(i*m/target_n) and sum? Use block: counts target groups, sum p within group; q = group_sums
  q=np.average? We'll use resampling: weights = floor(i*m/target_n) ranges.
```
Maybe not needed. Ensure after upsample, project to anti-pal if desired.

`random_antipal_x(n, rng)`:
Need generate x length m.
```
def random_antipal_x(n, rng):
  m=(n-1)//2 if n%2 else n//2
  if m<=0: return np.zeros(0)
  style = rng.integers(5)
  if style==0: return zeros
  idx=np.arange(n)
  if style < 3:
    # sine modes
    K = int(rng.integers(1, max(2, min(n, 20))))
    x=np.zeros(n)
    for _ in range(K):
       freq = int(rng.integers(1, n//2+1))
       amp = float(rng.uniform(-1.0,1.0))
       x += amp*np.sin(2*np.pi*freq*idx/n)
    # x length n anti; extract first m. For odd middle should zero but due float maybe; set x[(n-1)//2]=0
  else:
    # random anti noise maybe smooth via sine
    x=np.zeros(n)
    half = (n-1)//2 if n%2 else n//2
    first = rng.normal(size=half)
    # optionally apply random sine filter: lowpass by taking first few Fourier? Simpler random normal high freq.
    x[:half]=first
    if n%2==0: x[half:] = -first[::-1]
    else: x[half]=0; x[half+1:] = -first[::-1]
  # Extract pair x variables for first m: For even, x length n anti; var x_pair = x[:m]. For odd, x[:m]
  xpair = x[:m].copy()
  # Normalize/clamp
  mx = np.max(np.abs(xpair))
  if mx > 0:
     amp = float(rng.uniform(0.3,0.95))
     xpair = xpair / mx * amp
  return np.clip(xpair, -1.0,1.0)
```
For sine modes with multiple frequencies, xpair range maybe amp sum, normalize. Good.
Could also generate xpair directly anti: first half random and anti.

`optimize_antipal(x, n, deadline, rng, verbose=False)`:
Let's flesh carefully.
```
def optimize_antipal(x0, n, deadline, rng, beta_start=0.5, beta_end=30.0, alpha_init=0.1, maxalpha=0.5, minalpha=1e-4, max_iters=100000):
    m = n//2 if n%2==0 else (n-1)//2
    x = np.clip(np.asarray(x0,dtype=float).copy(), -1,1)
    if x.size != m: resize?
    a = make_antipal_sequence(x,n)
    best_a = a.copy()
    best_score = score_fast(a)
    best_x = x.copy()
    beta = n*beta_start
    alpha = alpha_init
    fail=0
    it=0
    start=time.monotonic()
    elapsed_total = max(1, deadline-start)
    total_time = float(max(delta?)) # pass total_budget? We can use progress in iterations to schedule beta.
    # To schedule beta, use max_iter maybe. If no max_iter, use time with planned duration? We can pass duration.
    duration = max(1.0, deadline - start)
    beta = ...
    while time.monotonic() < deadline and it < max_iters:
       progress = min(1.0, (time.monotonic()-start)/max(1.0,duration))
       beta = n*(beta_start + (beta_end-beta_start)*progress) # maybe ease
       c,obj,w = smooth_max(a,beta)
       if not np.isfinite(obj): break
       ga = gradient_from_weights(a,w)
       if m>0:
          if n%2==0:
             gx = (ga[:m] - ga[m:])/n  # derivative wrt x (without /? check: a_i=(1+x_i)/n; deriv wrt x_i: d_i/n. Pair j has deriv -d_j/n. So yes.
          else:
             gx = (ga[:m] - ga[m+1:][::-1]?)/n
```
Careful: For odd, x variables correspond a[:m]=(1+x)/n and a[m+1:]=(1-x[::-1])/n. For pair i variable x_i, a_i derivative +1/n, a_{n-1-i} derivative -1/n. The array `second = a[m+1:][::-1]` corresponds indices n-1 down to m+1, so pair i j. ga pair = ga[n-1-i] = ga[second][i]. So gx=(ga[:m]-ga[m+1:][::-1])/n. Good.
For even, second = ga[m:][::-1] corresponds n-1..m. So gx=(ga[:m]-ga[m:][::-1])/n. (Earlier wrote ga[m:] without reverse? Let's check: ga[m:] indices m..n-1, we need reverse. Use second = ga[m:][::-1].)
So for even: gx=(ga[:m]-ga[m:][::-1])/n.
Odd: gx=(ga[:m]-ga[m+1:][::-1])/n.
       nm = np.max(np.abs(gx))
       if nm > 1e-15:
          direction = -gx/nm
       else:
          direction = rng.normal(size=m)
       accepted=False
       # Try candidate alpha sequence: [alpha, alpha*0.5,...] until min
       a_trial_step = alpha
       for _ in range(6):
           xp = np.clip(x + a_trial_step*direction, -1.0,1.0)
           # If no change (within tiny), reduce? 
           if np.allclose(xp, x, rtol=0, atol=1e-15):
               a_trial_step *=0.5
               continue
           ap = make_antipal_sequence(xp,n)
           c2,obj2,w2=smooth_max(ap,beta)
           if np.isfinite(obj2) and obj2 < obj - 1e-12:
               x=xp; a=ap; obj=obj2; c=c2; w=w2
               accepted=True
               alpha = min(maxalpha, alpha*1.05)
               fail=0
               break
           a_trial_step *=0.5
           if a_trial_step < minalpha: break
       if not accepted:
          fail +=1
          alpha = max(minalpha, alpha*0.5)
          if fail > 30:
              # add random noise to x and reset alpha
              noise_scale = 0.05 if progress<0.8 else 0.02
              x = np.clip(x + noise_scale*rng.normal(size=m), -1,1)
              a = make_antipal_sequence(x,n)
              # recompute? next loop will
              alpha = alpha_init*0.5
              fail=0
       it +=1
       if it % 10 ==0:
           sc = score_fast(a)
           if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score - 1e-12:
               best_score=sc; best_a=a.copy(); best_x=x.copy()
       # optional exact evaluate not here
    return best_a, best_score, best_x
```
Potential inefficiency: `smooth_max` computes conv; `score_fast` also computes conv. Could reuse c from current a for score? Since c from smooth_max is full c. Objective score=2n max(c)/sum^2 (sum1). We can use `mc = c.max()` at intervals instead of score_fast. For non-anti not sum? sum1. Use c, so no extra FFT. Do:
```
if it %5==0:
   mc=float(c.max()); sc=2*n*mc  # since sum1
   if sc<best_score: update
```
Need ensure c from current a; yes. But after updates, c current. If noise changed a without recomputing, next loop c fresh; okay. We can compute at end of loop with c. Good. For initial best, compute via score_fast or c.

We should recompute best_obj? obj is smooth. If fail and noise changes a, old c not valid but next loop updates.

What if anti-pal a sum exactly1 but make_antipal after clip x? x clipped ensures nonnegative; sum exactly. Good.

def local_refine_full(a0, deadline, rng):
   a=np.asarray(a0,dtype=float).copy(); a=np.maximum(a,0); s=a.sum(); if s<=0: a uniform; else a/=s
   score=score_fast(a); best=a.copy(); best_score=score
   n=a.size
   while time<deadline:
      # generate candidate based on a (not best? Use best for search plateau)
      method = rng.integers(4)
      if method==0:
         cand=a.copy()
         i=int(rng.integers(n)); j=int(rng.integers(n)); if i==j continue
         frac=float(rng.uniform(0.01,0.5))
         move=frac*a[i]
         if move>0:
            cand[i]=max(0.0,a[i]-move); cand[j]=cand[j]+move
      elif method==1:
         cand=a.copy(); i,j; sigma=float(rng.uniform(0.05,0.5)); cand[i]*=np.exp(sigma); cand[j]*=np.exp(-sigma)
         sc=...
      elif method==2:
         cand=block perturbation as previous: width=... start; cand[start:stop]*=exp(normal); normalize
      elif method==3:
         cand=single coord scale
      cand/=cand.sum() if sum>0
      sc=score_fast(cand)
      if sc<best_score-1e-12:
         best=cand; best_score=sc; a=cand
      # every time no improvement many, reset a=best
   return best,best_score
```

```
def optimize_full(a0, deadline, rng, beta_start=0.5,beta_end=20.0):
   a = normalize(a0)
   n=a.size
   loga=np.log(np.maximum(a,1e-300)); loga-=np.max(loga)
   alpha=0.05*n? Wait log variables: update loga += step * direction where direction = -ga/nm? We can choose direction in loga units. ga scale ~1/n; normalize direction to max 1. Step in loga units (relative change). Let alpha=0.2 (log units). x_trial=loga + alpha*direction? For descent direction d=-ga? Usually update loga -= alpha*ga. If ga positive high, decrease loga. d = -ga/nm, so loga += alpha*d.
   line search alpha in [0.2,0.1,0.05...]. softmax to a_trial.
   objective smooth_max.
   On fail, noise.
```
This is similar but unconstrained. Derivative wrt loga = grad_a? Because loga variable change δx_i changes a_i by a_i δx_i (and normalization). // The gradient of f wrt log variables is a_i grad_i - a_i∑a_j grad_j? But using direction -grad_i may not be tangent? Let's derive: f scale-invariant and sum constraint. If we use log variables without explicit sum (softmax), the gradient is a_i grad_i - a_i μ where μ=∑ a_i grad_i. But Euler for homogeneous degree0: ∑a_i grad_i=0 (since f(ta)=f(a) derivative ->∑a_i grad_i=0) on the simplex scale? Actually f is scale invariant for any positive a; derivative wrt loga (adding constant to all loga scales a) is 0, so ∑a_i grad_i=0. Thus natural gradient wrt log variables is a_i grad_i (since μ=0). But if a normalized sum1, adding uniform log changes scale, f invariant; gradient should be zero along constant direction, but a_i grad_i sum=0, not componentwise zero. Wait if update loga by constant c, a scales by e^c, f unchanged. The directional derivative in loga constant direction is ∑ ∂f/∂log_i *1 = ∑ a_i grad_i =0. Good. So ∇_log = a_i grad_i. To do gradient descent in loga, use direction = -a_i grad_i. This is multiplicative relative. If we use -grad_i instead, not correct scale. But we can any direction as heuristic. Better use -a_i grad_i.
Thus direction = - (a * grad) normalized. Then loga += alpha*direction; softmax. This gives relative changes. Good.
For anti-pal x optimizer, variables are constrained differences, but gradient is as derived.

Full optimizer:
```
def optimize_full(a0, deadline, rng, beta_start=0.5,beta_end=30.0):
   n=len; a=normalize
   loga = np.log(a); loga -= np.mean(loga)
   best...
   alpha=0.2
   beta schedule
   while time:
      c,obj,w=smooth_max(a,beta)
      ga=gradient_from_weights(a,w)
      gl=a*ga
      # remove mean constant? Since sum a=1, ∑gl=0 (approx); but add constant irrelevant; subtract np.mean(gl?) to keep dynamic.
      nm=np.max(np.abs(gl))
      if nm<1e-15: noise
      direction=-gl/nm
      # line search alpha log units
      for step in alpha, alpha*0.5,...:
         lt=loga + step*direction
         lt-=np.max(lt)
         at=np.exp(lt); at/=at.sum()
         obj2=smooth_max(at,beta)
         if obj2<obj: accept
      if fail: noise loga
      every 10 score fast using c
```
This can use zeros? loga cannot handle zero; initial positive. If a has zeros, set min 1e-12. Final exact can have zeros from local. It may not exploit zeros well but can push small.

def normalize_positive(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s <= 1e-300:
       return np.full_like(a,1.0/max(1,a.size))
    a /= s
    return a
```

def random_antipal_x(n,rng):
  m=...
  if m==0 return zeros
  style=int(rng.integers(4))
  idx=np.arange(n,dtype=float)
  if style ==0:
     x=np.zeros(m)
  elif style==1:
     # few sine modes
     x_full=np.zeros(n)
     K=int(rng.integers(1,6))
     freqs = rng.choice(np.arange(1,n//2+1), size=K, replace=False) if n//2>=K
     amps = rng.uniform(-1,1,K)
     for f,a in zip(freqs,amps): x_full += a*np.sin(2π f idx/n)
     x=x_full[:m]
  elif style==2:
     # one dominant sine + noise
     f=int(rng.integers(1,max(2,n//2+1)))
     x_full=0.7*np.sin(2π f idx/n) + 0.3*rng.normal(n)
     # enforce anti: x_full[-1:i-1?]? We can do anti first half random:
     half=(n-1)//2 if n%2 else n//2
     noise=rng.normal(half); # combine
     x_pair=0.7*np.sin(2π f np.arange(half)/n)+0.3*noise
  else:
     # random first half smooth: Fourier lowpass
     half=m
     x=np.zeros(half)
     q=int(rng.integers(1, min(half,10)+1))?
     x[:] = rng.normal(half)
     # optional smooth by convolution? skip
```

```
def local_refine_full(a0, deadline, rng):
    a = normalize_positive(a0)
    best = a.copy(); best_score=score_fast(a); n=a.size; no_improve=0
    while time < deadline:
       cand = a.copy()
       method = int(rng.integers(4))
       if method ==0: # pair transfer
          i,j random; if i==j continue
          frac = rng.uniform(0.02,0.4)
          move=frac*a[i]
          cand[i]-=move
          if cand[i]<0: frac=-? move=a[i]
          cand[j]+=move
       elif method==1: # two-coordinate log scale
          i,j; sigma=rng.uniform(-0.3,0.3); cand[i]*=exp(sigma); cand[j]*=exp(-sigma)
       elif method==2: # block scale
          w=int(rng.integers(max(1,n//200), max(2,n//20)))
          s=int(rng.integers(0,n-w))
          factor=float(np.exp(rng.normal(0,0.15)))
          cand[s:s+w]*=factor
       elif method==3: # zero small / random coordinate zero
          zero_count = n//20? choose random block width maybe set to 0? We'll do set a random small block to 0 if sum positive.
          w=int(rng.integers(1, max(2,n//50)))
          s=int(rng.integers(0,n-w))
          cand[s:s+w]=0.0
       s=cand.sum(); if s<=0 continue; cand/=s
       sc=score_fast(cand)
       if sc < best_score - 1e-12:
          best=cand; best_score=sc; a=cand; no_improve=0
       else:
          a=best.copy() # reset
          no_improve+=1
          if no_improve>2000: reset a=best; no_improve=0
       # occasionally print
    return best,best_score
```

`next_power_of_two`:
```
def next_power_of_two(n):
    if n <= 1: return 1
    return 1 << (int(n)-1).bit_length()
```
Need n int. If n=2*4498-1=8995, need=8995, (8994).bit_length? Let's test: 1<< (n-1).bit_length() gives next power >= n for n>1. e.g. n=8 -> (7).bit_length=3 =>8. n=9 -> (8).bit_length=4=>16. Good.

`conv_square_fast`:
```
def conv_square_fast(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = a.size
    if n == 0: return np.array([], dtype=np.float64)
    if n <= 256:
        c = np.convolve(a, a)
    else:
        need = 2*n - 1
        L = next_power_of_two(need)
        Af = np.fft.rfft(a, L)
        c = np.fft.irfft(Af * Af, L)
        c = c[:need]
    c = np.asarray(c, dtype=np.float64)
    c = np.where(np.isfinite(c), c, 0.0)
    c = np.maximum(c, 0.0)
    return c
```
`np.maximum` with scalar? okay.

`score_fast`:
```
def score_fast(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a = np.maximum(a,0.0)
    n=a.size
    if n==0: return float('inf')
    s=float(a.sum())
    if s < 0.01: return float('inf')
    c=conv_square_fast(a)
    mc=float(c.max()) if c.size else 0.0
    if not np.isfinite(mc): return inf
    return float(2.0 * n * mc / (s*s))
```
Scale invariant; if values large >1000? But internal sum1.

`smooth_max`:
```
def smooth_max(a,beta):
    c=conv_square_fast(a)
    if not c.size: return c,0.0,np.array([1.0])
    mc=float(c.max())
    if mc <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(mc):
        w=np.full(c.size,1.0/c.size)
        return c,0.0,w
    beta=float(beta)
    x=beta*(c-mc)
    # clamp very negative to avoid under? exp handles.
    ex=np.exp(x)
    s=float(ex.sum())
    if s <=0 or not np.isfinite(s):
       w=np.zeros(c.size); intw=np.argmax(c); w[intw]=1.0; return c,mc,w
    w=ex/s
    obj=mc + float(np.log(s))/beta
    return c,obj,w
```
If beta very small, log(s) ~ log(len); okay.

`gradient_from_weights`:
```
def gradient_from_weights(a,w):
    a=np.asarray(a,float).ravel(); w=np.asarray(w,float).ravel()
    n=a.size; Lc=w.size
    if n==0: return np.array([])
    need=n+Lc-1
    L=next_power_of_two(need)
    Ar=np.fft.rfft(a[::-1], L)
    W=np.fft.rfft(w, L)
    g=np.fft.irfft(Ar*W, L)
    grad=2.0*g[n-1:2*n-1]
    grad=np.where(np.isfinite(grad), grad,0.0)
    return grad
```
Check slice length: g size L; n+m-1? For n=3,Lc=5, need7. n-1=2, 2*n-1=5 exclusive? Python slice [2:5] length3 (2,3,4). We need indices 2,3,4? i+n-1 for i=0..2 =>2,3,4. yes. Equivalent `: 2*n-1`. Good.

`normalize_positive`:
```
def normalize_positive(a):
    a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64).copy().ravel()
    a=np.where(np.isfinite(a),a,0.0)
    a=np.maximum(a,0.0)
    s=float(a.sum())
    if s <= 1e-300:
        return np.full(a.size, 1.0/max(1,a.size))
    a /= s
    return a
```

`make_antipal_sequence`: ensure x length. If x size shorter, pad? We can handle:
```
def make_antipal_sequence(x,n):
    x=np.asarray(x,dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    m=n//2 if n%2==0 else (n-1)//2
    if x.size != m:
       xx=np.zeros(m)
       k=min(m,x.size); xx[:k]=x[:k]; x=xx
    x=np.clip(x,-1.0,1.0)
    a=np.empty(n,float)
    if n%2==0:
       a[:m]=(1.0+x)/n
       a[m:]=(1.0-x[::-1])/n
    else:
       a[:m]=(1.0+x)/n
       a[m]=1.0/n
       a[m+1:]=(1.0-x[::-1])/n
    a=np.maximum(a,0.0)
    return a
```
If n=1, m=0, odd: a[0]=1.0. Good.

`project_to_antipal_x`:
```
def project_to_antipal_x(a,n):
    a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if a.size != n:
       # adjust
       a = upsample_sequence(a,n)
    m=n//2 if n%2==0 else (n-1)//2
    if m==0: return np.array([],float)
    first=a[:m]
    stop=n-m-1
    second=a[n-1:stop:-1]
    # Check length: for n=4,m=2, stop=1, slice 3:1:-1 length2. for n=1,m0 no.
    diff=first-second
    x=n*0.5*diff
    x=np.clip(x,-1.0,1.0)
    return x
```
For n=5,m=2, stop=2, slice 4:2:-1 length2 [4,3]. good.
For n=2,m=1, stop=0, slice1:0:-1 length1 [1]. good.

`upsample_sequence`:
```
def upsample_sequence(a, target_n):
    a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    m=a.size
    if m==0 or target_n<=0:
       return np.ones(target_n)/max(1,target_n)
    if target_n == m:
       return normalize_positive(a)
    if target_n > m:
       base = target_n // m
       rem = target_n % m
       if base == 0:
          # assign each one, some repeated? not needed
       q=np.empty(target_n)
       pos=0
       for j in range(m):
           cnt = base + (1 if j < rem else 0)
           if cnt <=0: continue
           if a[j] >0:
               q[pos:pos+cnt] = a[j]/cnt
           else:
               q[pos:pos+cnt] = 0.0
           pos += cnt
       return normalize_positive(q)
    else:
       # downsample by binning
       edges=np.linspace(0,m,target_n+1).astype(np.int64)
       # ensure unique? If target_n>m handled; here target_n<m; edges may repeat.
       q=np.zeros(target_n)
       for i in range(target_n):
           s=int(edges[i]); e=int(edges[i+1])
           if e>s:
              q[i]=float(a[s:e].sum())
       return normalize_positive(q)
```
If target_n > m and base=0 impossible because target>m so base≥1. Good.
If a zeros, normalize positive; if all zero, uniform.

`random_antipal_x`:
```
def random_antipal_x(n,rng):
    m = n//2 if n%2==0 else (n-1)//2
    if m<=0: return np.array([],float)
    style=int(rng.integers(4))
    idx=np.arange(m,dtype=np.float64)
    x=np.zeros(m)
    maxfreq = max(1, n//2)
    if style == 0:
       return x
    elif style == 1:
       K=int(rng.integers(1, min(8, m)+1))
       freqs = rng.integers(1, maxfreq+1, size=K)
       amps = rng.uniform(-1.0,1.0,size=K)
       for f,a in zip(freqs,amps):
           x += a*np.sin(2.0*np.pi*f*idx/n)
    elif style == 2:
       f=int(rng.integers(1, maxfreq+1))
       x += 0.8*np.sin(2.0*np.pi*f*idx/n)
       x += 0.3*rng.normal(size=m)
    else:
       x = rng.normal(size=m)
       # lowpass: keep first few sine modes via FFT? simple random
    mx=np.max(np.abs(x))
    if mx > 1e-12:
       amp = float(rng.uniform(0.4,0.95))
       x = x*(amp/mx)
    return np.clip(x,-1.0,1.0)
```
`rng.integers` with size. Good.
`zip(freqs, amps)` okay. No lambda.

`lowfreq_antipal_x`:
```
def lowfreq_antipal_x(n, freq, amp):
    m=n//2 if n%2==0 else (n-1)//2
    if m<=0: return np.zeros(0)
    idx=np.arange(m)
    x=amp*np.sin(2*np.pi*freq*idx/n)
    return np.clip(x,-1,1)
```
If amp >1, sin max1 so range amp; if amp >1 clip. Use amp 0.5-0.95.

```
        beta = n * beta_factor
        c,obj,w = smooth_max(a,beta)
        if not np.isfinite(obj): break

        mc = float(c.max())
        sc = 2.0*n*mc # sum=1
        if sc < best_score - 1e-12:
            best_score=sc; best_a=a.copy(); best_x=x.copy()
        ga = gradient_from_weights(a,w)
        if n%2==0:
            second = ga[m:][::-1]
        else:
            second = ga[m+1:][::-1]
        gx = (ga[:m] - second)/n
        nm=float(np.max(np.abs(gx)))
        if nm > 1e-15:
            direction = -gx/nm
        else:
            direction = rng.normal(size=m)
            nm=1.0 # not used
        accepted=False
        step=alpha
        for _ in range(6):
            xp = x + step*direction
            xp = np.clip(xp, -1.0, 1.0)
            # if no change
            if np.max(np.abs(xp-x)) < 1e-15:
                step *=0.5
                if step < minalpha: break
                continue
            ap = make_antipal_sequence(xp,n)
            c2,obj2,w2=smooth_max(ap,beta)
            if np.isfinite(obj2) and obj2 < obj - 1e-12:
                x=xp; a=ap; obj=obj2; c=c2; w=w2
                accepted=True
                alpha=min(maxalpha, alpha*1.1)
                fail=0
                break
            step *=0.5
            if step < minalpha: break
        if not accepted:
            fail +=1
            alpha=max(minalpha, alpha*0.6)
            if fail > 40:
                noise=0.05 if progress < 0.7 else 0.02
                x = np.clip(x + noise*rng.normal(size=m), -1.0,1.0)
                a = make_antipal_sequence(x,n)
                alpha=alpha_init*0.5
                fail=0
        it +=1
    # final score
    sc=score_fast(a) maybe use best
    return best_a, best_score, best_x
```

def local_refine_full(a0, deadline, rng, max_print=10):
    a=normalize_positive(a0); best=a.copy(); best_score=score_fast(a); n=a.size
    start=time.monotonic(); fail=0
    it=0
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
       cand=best.copy()
       method=int(rng.integers(5))
       if method==0:
          i=int(rng.integers(n)); j=int(rng.integers(n))
          if i != j:
             frac=float(rng.uniform(0.02,0.4))
             move=frac*best[i]
             ci=best[i]-move
             if ci <0: move=best[i]; ci=0.0
             cand[i]=ci; cand[j]+=move
       elif method==1:
          i,j; sigma=float(rng.uniform(0.05,0.5)); cand[i]*=np.exp(sigma); cand[j]*=np.exp(-sigma)
       elif method==2:
          width=int(rng.integers(max(1,n//200), max(2,n//10+1)))? Need min/max avoid

          start=int(rng.integers(0, n-width+1))
          cand[start:start+width]=0.0
       else:
          # random shift of mass from one random to another block? maybe reverse block
          width=...
       s=float(cand.sum())
       if s<=1e-300: continue
       cand /= s
       sc=score_fast(cand)
       if sc < best_score - 1e-12:
           best=cand; best_score=sc; fail=0
       else:
           fail +=1
           if fail>1000:
               # try random restart? no, just reset fail and maybe noise
               fail=0
       it +=1
       # maybe print every 2000? avoid too much.
    return best,best_score

```
def lp_improve(a0, max_iters=20, deadline=time.monotonic()+30):
   a=normalize_positive(a0); n=a.size
   if n==0 or n>256: return a,score
   best=a; best_score=score_fast(a)
   for it in range(max_iters):
      if time.monotonic()>deadline: break
      c=conv_square_fast(a); M=float(c.max()); if M<=0: break
      # build A scaled by M? A dense
      L=2*n-1
      A=np.zeros((L,n),float)
      for k in range(L):
          st=max(0,k-n+1); en=min(n-1,k)+1
          A[k,st:en]=a[st:en]
      A /= M
      cvec=-np.ones(n)
      bvec=np.ones(L)
      try:
         res=linprog(cvec, A_ub=A, b_ub=bvec, bounds=[(0,None)]*n, method='highs')
      except: break
      if not res.success or res.x is None: break
      g=np.maximum(res.x,0)
      Sg=float(g.sum())
      if Sg <=1.0+1e-8: break
      # candidates
      improved=False
      # g normalized
      cand=normalize_positive(g)
      for blend in [1.0,0.5,0.25,0.1]:
         if blend<1:
             cand2=normalize_positive(a*(1-blend)+g*blend)
         else: cand2=cand
         sc=score_fast(cand2)
         if sc < best_score -1e-10:
             best=cand2; best_score=sc; a=cand2; improved=True; break
      if not improved:
         # maybe use g to perturb? break
         break
   return best,best_score
```

```python
"""
Searches for low-peak nonnegative sequences minimizing
    2*n*max(conv(a,a))/(sum(a))^2.

Method:
- Optimize anti-palindromic sequences (pair sums fixed, values in [0,2/n])
  by projected gradient descent on a smoothed maximum of the convolution.
- Generate small-scale seeds, improve them with full-space log-gradient steps
  and small dense LP packing steps when possible, then upscale to the target
  construction length.
- Refine the large target sequence with both anti-palindromic and full-space
  gradient steps, then finish with random local refinement.
"""

import time
import numpy as np

try:
    from scipy.optimize import linprog
    _HAVE_LINPROG = True
except Exception:
    _HAVE_LINPROG = False


def next_power_of_two(n):
    n = int(n)
    if n <= 1:
        return 1
    return 1 << (n - 1).bit_length()


def normalize_positive(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel().copy()
    if a.size == 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=np.float64)
    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s <= 1e-300:
        return np.full(a.size, 1.0 / max(1, a.size), dtype=np.float64)
    a /= s
    return a


def conv_square_fast(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=np.float64)
    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)

    if n <= 256:
        c = np.convolve(a, a)
    else:
        need = 2 * n - 1
        L = next_power_of_two(need)
        af = np.fft.rfft(a, L)
        c = np.fft.irfft(af * af, L)
        c = c[:need]

    c = np.asarray(c, dtype=np.float64)
    c = np.where(np.isfinite(c), c, 0.0)
    c = np.maximum(c, 0.0)
    return c


def score_fast(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return float("inf")
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s < 0.01:
        return float("inf")
    c = conv_square_fast(a)
    if c.size == 0:
        return float("inf")
    mc = float(c.max())
    if not np.isfinite(mc):
        return float("inf")
    return float(2.0 * n * mc / (s * s))


def smooth_max(a, beta):
    c = conv_square_fast(a)
    if c.size == 0:
        return c, 0.0, np.array([1.0])

    mc = float(c.max())
    if mc <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(mc):
        w = np.full(c.size, 1.0 / c.size, dtype=np.float64)
        return c, 0.0, w

    beta = float(beta)
    x = beta * (c - mc)
    ex = np.exp(x)
    s = float(ex.sum())
    if s <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(s):
        w = np.zeros(c.size, dtype=np.float64)
        w[int(np.argmax(c))] = 1.0
        return c, mc, w

    w = ex / s
    obj = mc + float(np.log(s)) / beta
    return c, obj, w


def gradient_from_weights(a, w):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    w = np.asarray(w, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = a.size
    Lc = w.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=np.float64)

    need = n + Lc - 1
    L = next_power_of_two(need)
    ar = np.fft.rfft(a[::-1], L)
    wf = np.fft.rfft(w, L)
    g = np.fft.irfft(ar * wf, L)
    grad = 2.0 * g[n - 1:2 * n - 1]
    grad = np.where(np.isfinite(grad), grad, 0.0)
    return grad


def make_antipal_sequence(x, n):
    n = int(n)
    x = np.asarray(x, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if n % 2 == 0:
        m = n // 2
    else:
        m = (n - 1) // 2

    if x.size != m:
        xx = np.zeros(m, dtype=np.float64)
        k = min(m, x.size)
        if k > 0:
            xx[:k] = x[:k]
        x = xx

    x = np.clip(x, -1.0, 1.0)
    a = np.empty(n, dtype=np.float64)

    if n % 2 == 0:
        a[:m] = (1.0 + x) / float(n)
        a[m:] = (1.0 - x[::-1]) / float(n)
    else:
        a[:m] = (1.0 + x) / float(n)
        a[m] = 1.0 / float(n)
        a[m + 1:] = (1.0 - x[::-1]) / float(n)

    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    return a


def project_to_antipal_x(a, n):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = int(n)
    if n % 2 == 0:
        m = n // 2
    else:
        m = (n - 1) // 2

    if m == 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=np.float64)

    if a.size != n:
        a = upsample_sequence(a, n)
    else:
        a = normalize_positive(a)

    first = a[:m]
    stop = n - m - 1
    second = a[n - 1:stop:-1]
    second = np.where(np.isfinite(second), second, 0.0)

    x = 0.5 * float(n) * (first - second)
    x = np.where(np.isfinite(x), x, 0.0)
    return np.clip(x, -1.0, 1.0)


def upsample_sequence(a, target_n):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    target_n = int(target_n)
    if target_n <= 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=np.float64)

    a = normalize_positive(a)
    m = a.size
    if m == 0:
        return np.ones(target_n, dtype=np.float64) / float(target_n)
    if target_n == m:
        return a

    if target_n > m:
        base = target_n // m
        rem = target_n % m
        q = np.empty(target_n, dtype=np.float64)
        pos = 0
        for j in range(m):
            cnt = base + (1 if j < rem else 0)
            if cnt <= 0:
                continue
            val = a[j] / float(cnt)
            if not np.isfinite(val) or val <= 0.0:
                val = 0.0
            q[pos:pos + cnt] = val
            pos += cnt
        return normalize_positive(q)

    edges = np.linspace(0, m, target_n + 1).astype(np.int64)
    q = np.zeros(target_n, dtype=np.float64)
    for i in range(target_n):
        s = int(edges[i])
        e = int(edges[i + 1])
        if e > s:
            q[i] = float(np.sum(a[s:e]))
    return normalize_positive(q)


def random_antipal_x(n, rng):
    n = int(n)
    if n % 2 == 0:
        m = n // 2
    else:
        m = (n - 1) // 2

    if m <= 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=np.float64)

    style = int(rng.integers(4))
    idx = np.arange(m, dtype=np.float64)
    x = np.zeros(m, dtype=np.float64)
    maxfreq = max(1, n // 2)

    if style == 0:
        return x

    if style == 1:
        k = int(rng.integers(1, min(8, m) + 1))
        freqs = rng.integers(1, maxfreq + 1, size=k)
        amps = rng.uniform(-1.0, 1.0, size=k)
        for f, amp in zip(freqs, amps):
            x += float(amp) * np.sin(2.0 * np.pi * float(f) * idx / float(n))
    elif style == 2:
        f = int(rng.integers(1, maxfreq + 1))
        x += 0.8 * np.sin(2.0 * np.pi * float(f) * idx / float(n))
        x += 0.3 * rng.normal(size=m)
    else:
        x = rng.normal(size=m)

    mx = float(np.max(np.abs(x)))
    if mx > 1e-12:
        amp = float(rng.uniform(0.4, 0.95))
        x = x * (amp / mx)

    return np.clip(x, -1.0, 1.0)


def lowfreq_antipal_x(n, freq, amp):
    n = int(n)
    if n % 2 == 0:
        m = n // 2
    else:
        m = (n - 1) // 2
    if m <= 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=np.float64)

    idx = np.arange(m, dtype=np.float64)
    x = float(amp) * np.sin(2.0 * np.pi * float(freq) * idx / float(n))
    return np.clip(x, -1.0, 1.0)


def square_antipal_x(n, freq, amp):
    n = int(n)
    if n % 2 == 0:
        m = n // 2
    else:
        m = (n - 1) // 2
    if m <= 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=np.float64)

    idx = np.arange(m, dtype=np.float64)
    x = np.sign(np.sin(2.0 * np.pi * float(freq) * idx / float(n))) * float(amp)
    x = np.where(np.isfinite(x), x, 0.0)
    return np.clip(x, -1.0, 1.0)


def optimize_antipal(x0, n, deadline, rng,
                     beta_start=0.3, beta_end=30.0,
                     alpha_init=0.12, maxalpha=0.8, minalpha=1e-5,
                     max_iters=10000000):
    n = int(n)
    if n % 2 == 0:
        m = n // 2
    else:
        m = (n - 1) // 2

    if m == 0:
        a = make_antipal_sequence(np.array([], dtype=np.float64), n)
        return a, score_fast(a), np.array([], dtype=np.float64)

    xsrc = np.asarray(x0, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    x = np.zeros(m, dtype=np.float64)
    k = min(m, xsrc.size)
    if k > 0:
        x[:k] = xsrc[:k]
    x = np.clip(x, -1.0, 1.0)

    if float(np.max(np.abs(x))) < 1e-7:
        x = random_antipal_x(n, rng)

    a = make_antipal_sequence(x, n)
    best_a = a.copy()
    best_score = score_fast(a)
    best_x = x.copy()

    start = time.monotonic()
    duration = max(1e-6, float(deadline) - start)
    alpha = float(alpha_init)
    fail = 0
    it = 0

    while time.monotonic() < deadline and it < max_iters:
        progress = (time.monotonic() - start) / duration
        if progress < 0.0:
            progress = 0.0
        if progress > 1.0:
            progress = 1.0
        t = float(progress) ** 0.5
        beta_factor = float(beta_start) + (float(beta_end) - float(beta_start)) * t
        beta = float(n) * beta_factor

        c, obj, w = smooth_max(a, beta)
        if not np.isfinite(obj):
            break

        mc = float(c.max())
        sc = float(2.0) * float(n) * mc
        if sc < best_score - 1e-12:
            best_score = sc
            best_a = a.copy()
            best_x = x.copy()

        ga = gradient_from_weights(a, w)

        if n % 2 == 0:
            second = ga[m:][::-1]
        else:
            second = ga[m + 1:][::-1]

        gx = (ga[:m] - second) / float(n)
        nm = float(np.max(np.abs(gx)))
        if nm > 1e-15:
            direction = -gx / nm
        else:
            direction = rng.normal(size=m)

        accepted = False
        step = alpha
        for _ in range(6):
            xp = np.clip(x + step * direction, -1.0, 1.0)
            if float(np.max(np.abs(xp - x))) < 1e-15:
                step *= 0.5
                if step < minalpha:
                    break
                continue

            ap = make_antipal_sequence(xp, n)
            c2, obj2, _ = smooth_max(ap, beta)
            if np.isfinite(obj2) and obj2 < obj - 1e-12:
                x = xp
                a = ap
                obj = obj2
                c = c2
                accepted = True
                alpha = min(maxalpha, alpha * 1.1)
                fail = 0
                break

            step *= 0.5
            if step < minalpha:
                break

        if not accepted:
            fail += 1
            alpha = max(minalpha, alpha * 0.6)
            if fail > 40:
                noise = 0.05 if progress < 0.7 else 0.02
                x = np.clip(x + noise * rng.normal(size=m), -1.0, 1.0)
                a = make_antipal_sequence(x, n)
                alpha = max(minalpha, alpha_init * 0.5)
                fail = 0

        it += 1

    final_score = score_fast(best_a)
    if final_score < best_score:
        best_score = final_score
        best_a = a.copy()
        best_x = x.copy()

    return best_a, best_score, best_x


def optimize_full(a0, deadline, rng,
                  beta_start=0.3, beta_end=30.0,
                  alpha_init=0.15, maxalpha=0.8, minalpha=1e-5,
                  max_iters=10000000):
    a = normalize_positive(a0)
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return a, float("inf")

    floor = 1e-12 / float(max(1, n))
    a = np.maximum(a, floor)
    a = normalize_positive(a)

    loga = np.log(a)
    loga -= float(np.max(loga))

    best_a = a.copy()
    best_score = score_fast(a)

    start = time.monotonic()
    duration = max(1e-6, float(deadline) - start)
    alpha = float(alpha_init)
    fail = 0
    it = 0

    while time.monotonic() < deadline and it < max_iters:
        progress = (time.monotonic() - start) / duration
        if progress < 0.0:
            progress = 0.0
        if progress > 1.0:
            progress = 1.0
        t = float(progress) ** 0.5
        beta_factor = float(beta_start) + (float(beta_end) - float(beta_start)) * t
        beta = float(n) * beta_factor

        c, obj, w = smooth_max(a, beta)
        if not np.isfinite(obj):
            break

        s = float(a.sum())
        mc = float(c.max())
        sc = (2.0 * float(n) * mc) / (s * s) if s > 0.0 else float("inf")
        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score - 1e-12:
            best_score = sc
            best_a = a.copy()

        ga = gradient_from_weights(a, w)
        gl = a * ga
        gl -= float(np.mean(gl))
        gl = np.where(np.isfinite(gl), gl, 0.0)

        nm = float(np.max(np.abs(gl)))
        if nm > 1e-15:
            direction = -gl / nm
        else:
            direction = rng.normal(size=n)

        accepted = False
        step = alpha
        for _ in range(6):
            lt = loga + step * direction
            lt -= float(np.max(lt))
            at = np.exp(lt)
            s2 = float(at.sum())
            if s2 <= 1e-300 or not np.isfinite(s2):
                step *= 0.5
                continue
            at /= s2
            at = np.maximum(at, 0.0)

            _, obj2, _ = smooth_max(at, beta)
            if np.isfinite(obj2) and obj2 < obj - 1e-12:
                loga = lt
                a = at
                obj = obj2
                c = cup if False else c  # placeholder to avoid unused? no
                accepted = True
                alpha = min(maxalpha, alpha * 1.1)
                fail = 0
                break

            step *= 0.5
            if step < minalpha:
                break

        if not accepted:
            fail += 1
            alpha = max(minalpha, alpha * 0.6)
            if fail > 50:
                loga = loga + 0.1 * rng.normal(size=n)
                loga -= float(np.max(loga))
                a = np.exp(loga)
                s3 = float(a.sum())
                if s3 > 0.0 and np.isfinite(s3):
                    a /= s3
                else:
                    a = normalize_positive(a)
                alpha = max(minalpha, alpha_init * 0.5)
                fail = 0

        it += 1

    final_score = score_fast(best_a)
    if final_score < best_score:
        best_score = final_score
        best_a = a.copy()

    return best_a, best_score


def lp_improve(a0, max_iters=8, deadline=None):
    a = normalize_positive(a0)
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return a, float("inf")
    if not _HAVE_LINPROG:
        return a, score_fast(a)
    if n > 128:
        return a, score_fast(a)

    if deadline is None:
        deadline = time.monotonic() + 1.0

    best = a.copy()
    best_score = score_fast(a)

    for _ in range(int(max_iters)):
        if time.monotonic() > deadline:
            break

        c = conv_square_fast(best)
        M = float(c.max())
        if M <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(M):
            break

        L = 2 * n - 1
        A = np.zeros((L, n), dtype=np.float64)
        for k in range(L):
            st = max(0, k - n + 1)
            en = min(n - 1, k) + 1
            A[k, st:en] = best[st:en]

        A = A / M
        cvec = -np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
        bvec = np.ones(L, dtype=np.float64)
        bounds = [(0.0, None)] * n

        try:
            res = linprog(cvec, A_ub=A, b_ub=bvec, bounds=bounds, method="highs")
        except Exception:
            break

        if not getattr(res, "success", False):
            break
        g = np.asarray(res.x, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
        g = np.maximum(g, 0.0)
        g = np.where(np.isfinite(g), g, 0.0)

        Sg = float(g.sum())
        if Sg <= 1.0 + 1e-8:
            break

        improved = False
        for blend in (1.0, 0.6, 0.35, 0.15):
            if blend >= 1.0:
                cand = normalize_positive(g)
            else:
                cand = normalize_positive((1.0 - blend) * best + blend * g)
            sc = score_fast(cand)
            if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score - 1e-10:
                best = cand
                best_score = sc
                improved = True
                break

        if not improved:
            break

    return best, best_score


def local_refine_full(a0, deadline, rng):
    a = normalize_positive(a0)
    n = a.size
    if n <= 1:
        return a, score_fast(a)

    best = a.copy()
    best_score = score_fast(a)

    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        cand = best.copy()
        method = int(rng.integers(5))

        if method == 0:
            i = int(rng.integers(n))
            j = int(rng.integers(n))
            if i != j:
                frac = float(rng.uniform(0.02, 0.45))
                move = frac * best[i]
                if move > 0.0:
                    ni = best[i] - move
                    if ni < 0.0:
                        ni = 0.0
                        move = best[i]
                    cand[i] = ni
                    cand[j] += move
        elif method == 1:
            i = int(rng.integers(n))
            j = int(rng.integers(n))
            if i != j:
                sigma = float(rng.uniform(0.05, 0.55))
                cand[i] *= float(np.exp(sigma))
                cand[j] *= float(np.exp(-sigma))
        elif method == 2:
            lo = max(1, n // 300)
            hi = max(lo, n // 12)
            width = int(rng.integers(lo, hi + 1))
            if width >= n:
                width = max(1, n // 100)
            if n - width > 0:
                st = int(rng.integers(0, n - width + 1))
            else:
                st = 0
            factor = float(np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, 0.22)))
            cand[st:st + width] *= factor
        elif method == 3:
            lo = max(1, n // 250)
            hi = max(lo, n // 25)
            width = int(rng.integers(lo, hi + 1))
            if width >= n:
                width = max(1, n // 100)
            if n - width > 0:
                st = int(rng.integers(0, n - width + 1))
            else:
                st = 0
            cand[st:st + width] = 0.0
        else:
            i = int(rng.integers(n))
